          <p>(3) authorizes the lessor to exercise the rights of a secured party with respect to the equipment subject to the lease in the event of default by the lessee and, in addition, for the present recovery of lease rentals due during the current term of the lease as liquidated damages.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Master lease-purchase agreements not debt.</span></h2>
          <p>A financing agreement under this section does not constitute or create a general or moral obligation or indebtedness of the state in excess of the money from time to time appropriated or otherwise available for the payment of rent coming due under the lease, and the state has no continuing obligation to appropriate money for the payment of rent or other obligations under the agreement. Rent due under a financing agreement under this section during a current term for which money has been appropriated is a current expense of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.4c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4c.<span class="headnote">Budget offset.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall require a state agency that uses the state energy improvement program to certify that the agency will budget, allocate, and commit agency funds sufficient to make rent payments under a financing agreement until all rent obligations are paid in full. In the event a participating agency fails to make a rent payment, the commissioner of management and budget shall reduce the operating budget of the state agency. The amount of the reduction is the amount sufficient to make the actual payments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Qualifying energy improvement projects.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may approve an energy improvement project for a financing agreement if the commissioner determines that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the project and project financing agreement have been approved by the governing body or head of the state agency that operates or manages the state building or facility to be improved;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the project is technically and economically feasible;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the state agency that operates or manages the state building or facility has made adequate provision for the operation and maintenance of the project;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) if an energy efficiency improvement, the project is calculated to result in a positive cash flow in each year the financing agreement is in effect;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the project proposer has fully explored the use of conservation investment plan opportunities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a> with the utilities providing gas and electric service to the energy improvement project;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) if a renewable energy improvement, the project is calculated to reduce use of fossil-fuel energy; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) if a geothermal energy improvement, the project is calculated to produce savings in terms of nongeothermal energy and costs.</p>
          <p class="bl">For the purpose of clause (6), "renewable energy" is energy produced by an eligible energy technology as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.1691#stat.216B.1691.1">216B.1691, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Program costs.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Program costs incurred by the commissioner or a state agency that are not reimbursed or paid directly under a financing agreement may be paid with money made available to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.43#stat.216C.43.10">216C.43, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Conservation investment plan savings goals.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A utility or association may count toward its energy-savings goals under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.1c">216B.241, subdivision 1c</a>, the energy savings resulting from its investment in an energy improvement project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.322.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Beginning January 15, 2009, and each year thereafter, the commissioner of administration shall submit to the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees on energy finance a report containing, at a minimum, the following information regarding projects implemented under this section:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total number of projects;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the amount of calculated and, if available, actual energy savings for each project;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the cost of each project; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the total amount paid for technical services provided under subdivision 3 for each project.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.322.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/356/#laws.0.2.0">2008 c 356 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/138/#laws.2.9.0">2009 c 138 art 2 s 9</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/369/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 369 s 2</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/7/#laws.0.32.0">2023 c 7 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.323"><b>16B.323</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.133.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 133</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.325">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.325 SUSTAINABLE BUILDING GUIDELINES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.325.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Development of sustainable building guidelines.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The Department of Administration and the Department of Commerce, with the assistance of other agencies, shall develop sustainable building design guidelines for all new state buildings by January 15, 2003, and for all major renovations of state buildings by February 1, 2009. The primary objectives of these guidelines are to ensure that all new state buildings, and major renovations of state buildings, initially exceed the state energy code, as established in Minnesota Rules, chapter 7676, by at least 30 percent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.325.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Lowest possible cost; energy conservation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The guidelines must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) focus on achieving the lowest possible lifetime cost, considering both construction and operating costs, for new buildings and major renovations;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) allow for revisions that encourage continual energy conservation improvements in new buildings and major renovations;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) define "major renovations" for purposes of this section to encompass not less than 10,000 square feet or not less than the replacement of the mechanical, ventilation, or cooling system of a building or a building section;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) establish sustainability guidelines that include air quality and lighting standards and that create and maintain a healthy environment and facilitate productivity improvements;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) establish resiliency guidelines to encourage design that allows buildings to adapt to and accommodate projected climate-related changes that are reflected in both acute events and chronic trends, including but not limited to changes in temperature and precipitation levels;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) specify ways to reduce material costs; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) consider the long-term operating costs of the building, including the use of renewable energy sources and distributed electric energy generation that uses a renewable source or natural gas or a fuel that is as clean or cleaner than natural gas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.325.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Development of guidelines; applicability.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In developing the guidelines, the departments shall use an open process, including providing the opportunity for public comment. The guidelines established under this section are mandatory for all new buildings receiving funding from the bond proceeds fund after January 1, 2004, and for all major renovations receiving funding from the bond proceeds fund after January 1, 2009.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.325.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Guideline revisions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioners of administration and commerce shall review the guidelines periodically and as soon as practicable revise the guidelines to incorporate performance standards developed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241#stat.216B.241.9">216B.241, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.325.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/212/#laws.1.2.0">2001 c 212 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.30.0">2008 c 179 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/278/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 278 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/365/#laws.0.9.0">2008 c 365 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.12.2.0">2023 c 60 art 12 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.326"><b>16B.326</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.133.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 133</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.327">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.327 RECYCLING CONSTRUCTION AND DEMOLITION WASTE FROM STATE BUILDINGS; REQUIREMENT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall require in contracts for the construction, renovation, or demolition of a state building that the contractor and any subcontractor must divert from deposit in a landfill and must recycle at least 50 percent of the nonhazardous construction and demolition waste, measured by tonnage or volume, produced by the project or demonstrate that the waste was delivered to construction and demolition waste recycling facilities that maintain a 50 percent annual recycling rate. This requirement applies to a project to construct, renovate, or demolish a state building that receives funding from the bond proceeds fund after January 1, 2011, provided that:</p>
        <p>(1) the project is located within 40 miles of a construction and demolition waste recycling facility that meets the requirements of this section and can process the applicable building materials; and</p>
        <p>(2) for construction and renovation projects, funding from the bond proceeds fund is $5,000,000 or more.</p>
        <p>For the purposes of this section, "state building" means a building wholly owned or leased by a state agency, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, or the University of Minnesota.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.327.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/189/#laws.0.33.0">2010 c 189 s 33</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.328">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.328 OUTDOOR LIGHTING FIXTURES MODEL ORDINANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.328.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "energy conservation" means reducing energy use and includes: (i) using a light with lower wattage; and (ii) using devices such as time controls, motion detectors, or on and off switches that limit unnecessary use of lighting;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "cutoff luminaire" means a luminaire in which 2.5 percent or less of the lamp lumens are emitted above a horizontal plane through the luminaire's lowest part and ten percent or less of the lamp lumens are emitted at a vertical angle 80 degrees above the luminaire's lowest point;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) "light pollution" means the shining of light produced by a luminaire above the height of the luminaire and into the sky;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) "lumen" means a unit of luminous flux. One footcandle is one lumen per square foot. For purposes of this section, the lumen-output values are the initial lumen-output rating of the lamp;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) "luminaire" means a complete lighting unit consisting of a light source and all necessary mechanical, electrical, and decorative parts; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) "outdoor lighting fixture" means any type of fixed or movable lighting equipment that is designed or used for illumination outdoors. The term includes billboard lighting, streetlights, searchlights, and other lighting used for advertising purposes and area lighting. The term does not include lighting equipment that is required by law to be installed on motor vehicles or lighting required for the safe operation of aircraft.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.328.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Model ordinance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of administration, in consultation with the commissioner of commerce, associations for local governments, and any other interested person, shall develop a model ordinance that can be adapted for use by cities, counties, and towns, governing outdoor lighting to reduce light pollution. The model ordinance must address:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) standards for lighting on private property; outdoor advertising; lighting on commercial, industrial, or institutional property; canopies covering fueling stations; and public streets, sidewalks, and alleys;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) how illumination levels should be measured;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) possible exemptions, such as for temporary emergency or hazard lighting;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) recommended elements for an exterior lighting plan for a development;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) treatment of nonconforming lighting;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) lighting standards that might apply in special subdistricts;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) light pole maximum heights; and</p>
          <p class="in">(8) light trespass.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.328.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Standards for state-funded outdoor lighting fixtures.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An outdoor lighting fixture may be installed or replaced using state funds only if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the new or replacement outdoor lighting fixture is a cutoff luminaire if the rated output of the outdoor lighting fixture is greater than 1,800 lumens;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the minimum illuminance adequate for the intended purpose is used with consideration given to nationally recognized standards;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) for lighting of a designated highway of the state highway system, the Department of Transportation determines that the purpose of the outdoor lighting fixture cannot be achieved by the installation of reflective road markers, lines, warning or informational signs, or other effective passive methods; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) full consideration has been given to energy conservation and savings, reducing glare, minimizing light pollution, and preserving the natural night environment.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a federal law, rule, or regulation preempts state law;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the outdoor lighting fixture is used on a temporary basis because emergency personnel require additional illumination for emergency procedures;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the outdoor lighting fixture is used on a temporary basis for nighttime work;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) special events or situations require additional illumination, provided that the illumination installed shields the outdoor lighting fixtures from direct view and minimizes upward lighting and light pollution;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the outdoor lighting fixture is used solely to highlight the aesthetic aspects of a single object or distinctive building; or</p>
          <p class="in">(6) a compelling safety interest exists that cannot be addressed by another method.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) This subdivision does not apply to the operation and maintenance of lights or lighting systems purchased or installed, or for which design work is completed, before August 1, 2008.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) This section does not apply if a state agency or local unit of government determines that compliance with this section would:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) require an increased use of electricity;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) increase the construction cost of a lighting system more than 15 percent over the construction cost of a lighting system that does not comply with this section;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) increase the cost of operation and maintenance of the lighting system more than ten percent over the cost of operating and maintaining the existing lighting system over the life of the lighting system; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) result in a negative safety impact.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.328.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.1.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/296/#laws.2.1.0">2008 c 296 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.33">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.33 DESIGNER SELECTION BOARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this section, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p>(b) "Agency" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.01">16B.01</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Architect" means an architect or landscape architect registered to practice under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326.02">326.02</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326.15">326.15</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Board" means the state Designer Selection Board.</p>
          <p>(e) "Design-build" means the process of entering into and managing a single contract between the commissioner and the design-builder in which the design-builder agrees to both design and construct a project as specified in the contract at a guaranteed maximum or a fixed price.</p>
          <p>(f) "Design-builder" means a person who proposes to design and construct a project in accordance with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.33">16C.33</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) "Designer" means an architect or engineer, or a partnership, association, or corporation comprised primarily of architects or engineers or of both architects and engineers.</p>
          <p>(h) "Engineer" means an engineer registered to practice under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326.02">326.02</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326.15">326.15</a>.</p>
          <p>(i) "Person" includes an individual, corporation, partnership, association, or any other legal entity.</p>
          <p>(j) "Primary designer" means the designer who is to have primary design responsibility for a project, and does not include designers who are merely consulted by the user agency and do not have substantial design responsibility, or designers who will or may be employed or consulted by the primary designer.</p>
          <p>(k) "Project" means an undertaking to construct, erect, or remodel a building by or for the state or an agency. Capital projects exempt from the requirements of this section include demolition or decommissioning of state assets; hazardous materials abatement; repair and replacement of utility infrastructure, parking lots, and parking structures; security upgrades; building systems replacement or repair, including alterations to building interiors needed to accommodate the systems; and other asset preservation work not involving remodeling of occupied space.</p>
          <p>(l) "User agency" means the agency undertaking a specific project. For projects undertaken by the state of Minnesota, "user agency" means the Department of Administration or a state agency with an appropriate delegation to act on behalf of the Department of Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Organization of board.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) <b>Membership. </b> The state Designer Selection Board consists of seven individuals, the majority of whom must be Minnesota residents. Each of the following four organizations shall nominate one individual whose name and qualifications shall be submitted to the commissioner of administration for consideration: the Consulting Engineers Council of Minnesota after consultation with other professional engineering societies in the state; the AIA Minnesota; the Minnesota chapter of the Associated General Contractors after consultation with other commercial contractor associations in the state; and the Minnesota Board of the Arts. The commissioner may appoint the four named individuals to the board but may reject a nominated individual and request another nomination. The fifth member shall be a representative of the user agency, the University of Minnesota, or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, designated by the user agency. The remaining two citizen members shall also be appointed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Nonvoting member.</b> In addition to the seven members of the board, one nonvoting member representing the commissioner shall participate in the interviewing and selection of designers pursuant to this section.</p>
          <p>(c) <b>Terms; compensation; removal; vacancies.</b> The membership terms, compensation, removal of members, and filling of vacancies on the board are as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. No individual may serve for more than two consecutive terms.</p>
          <p>(d) <b>Officers, rules.</b> At its first meeting, the board shall elect a voting member of the board as chair. The board shall also elect other officers necessary for the conduct of its affairs. The board shall adopt rules governing its operations and the conduct of its meetings. The rules shall provide for the terms of the chair and other officers.</p>
          <p>(e) <b>Meetings.</b> The board shall meet as often as is necessary, not less than twice annually, in order to act expeditiously on requests submitted to it for selection of primary designers.</p>
          <p>(f) <b>Office, staff, records.</b> The Department of Administration shall provide the board with suitable quarters to maintain an office, hold meetings, and keep records. The commissioner shall designate an employee of the Department of Administration to serve as executive secretary to the board and shall furnish a secretarial staff to the board as necessary for the expeditious conduct of the board's duties and responsibilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agencies must request designer.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) <b>Application.</b> Upon undertaking a project with an estimated cost greater than $4,000,000 or a planning project with estimated fees greater than $400,000, every user agency, except the Capitol Area Architectural and Planning Board, shall submit a written request for a primary designer for its project to the commissioner, who shall forward the request to the board. The University of Minnesota and the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities shall follow the process in subdivision 3a to select designers for their projects. The written request must include a description of the project, the estimated cost of completing the project, a description of any special requirements or unique features of the proposed project, and other information which will assist the board in carrying out its duties and responsibilities set forth in this section.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Reactivated project.</b> If a project for which a designer has been selected by the board becomes inactive, lapses, or changes as a result of project phasing, insufficient appropriations, or other reasons, the commissioner, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, or the University of Minnesota may, if the project is reactivated, retain the same designer to complete the project.</p>
          <p>(c) <b>Fee limit reached after designer selected.</b> If a project initially estimated to be below the cost and planning fee limits of this subdivision has its cost or planning fees revised so that the limits are exceeded, the project must be referred to the board for designer selection even if a primary designer has already been selected. In this event, the board may, without conducting interviews, elect to retain the previously selected designer if it determines that the interests of the state are best served by that decision and shall notify the commissioner of its determination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Higher education projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When the University of Minnesota or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities undertakes a project involving construction or major remodeling, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.335#stat.16B.335.1">16B.335, subdivision 1</a>, with an estimated cost greater than $4,000,000 or a planning project with estimated fees greater than $400,000, the system shall submit a written request for a primary designer to the commissioner, as provided in subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(b) When the University of Minnesota or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities undertakes a project involving renovation, repair, replacement, or rehabilitation, the system office may submit a written request for a primary designer to the commissioner as provided in subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(c) For projects at the University of Minnesota or the State Colleges and Universities, the board shall select at least two primary designers under subdivision 4 for recommendation to the Board of Regents or the Board of Trustees. Meeting records or written evaluations that document the final selection are public records. The Board of Regents or the Board of Trustees shall notify the commissioner of the designer selected from the recommendations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Designer selection process.</span></h2>
          <p> (a) <b>Publicity. </b> Upon receipt of a request from a user agency for a primary designer, the board shall publicize the proposed project in order to determine the identity of designers interested in the design work on the project. The board shall establish criteria for the selection process and make this information public, and shall compile data on and conduct interviews of designers. The board's selection criteria must include consideration of each interested designer's performance on previous projects for the state or any other person. Upon completing the process, the board shall select the primary designer and shall state its reasons in writing. If the board's vote for the selection of a primary designer results in a tie vote, the nonvoting member appointed under subdivision 2, paragraph (b), must vote for the selection of the primary designer. Notification to the commissioner of the selection shall be made not more than 60 days after receipt from a user agency of a request for a primary designer. The commissioner shall promptly notify the designer and the user agency. The commissioner shall negotiate the designer's fee and prepare the contract to be entered into between the designer and the user agency.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Conflict of interest.</b> A board member may not participate in the review, discussion, or selection of a designer or firm in which the member has a financial interest.</p>
          <p>(c) <b>Selection by commissioner.</b> In the event the board receives a request for a primary designer on a project, the estimated cost of which is less than the limit established by subdivision 3, or a planning project with estimated fees of less than the limit established by subdivision 3, the board may submit the request to the commissioner of administration, with or without recommendations, and the commissioner shall thereupon select the primary designer for the project.</p>
          <p>(d) <b>Second selection.</b> If the designer selected for a project declines the appointment or is unable to reach agreement with the commissioner on the fee or the terms of the contract, the commissioner shall, within 60 days after the first appointment, request the board to make another selection.</p>
          <p>(e) <b>Sixty days to select.</b> If the board fails to make a selection and forward its recommendation to the commissioner within 60 days of the user agency's request for a designer, the commissioner may appoint a designer to the project without the recommendation of the board.</p>
          <p>(f) <b>Less than satisfactory performance.</b> The commissioner, or the University of Minnesota and the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities for projects under their supervision, shall forward to the board a written report describing each instance in which the performance of a designer selected by the board or the commissioner has been less than satisfactory. Criteria for determining satisfaction include the ability of the designer to complete design work on time, to provide a design responsive to program needs within the constraints of the budget, to solve design problems and achieve a design consistent with the proposed function of the building, to avoid costly design errors or omissions, and to observe the construction work. These reports are public data and are available for inspection under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.03">13.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.33.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rate of inflation.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than December 31 of every fifth year starting in 2025, the commissioner shall determine the percentage increase in the rate of inflation, as measured by the Means Quarterly Construction Cost Index, during the four-year period preceding that year. The thresholds in subdivisions 3, paragraph (a); and 3a, paragraph (a), shall be increased by the percentage calculated by the commissioner to the nearest ten-thousandth dollar.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.38.0">1984 c 544 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/285/#laws.0.5.0">1985 c 285 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/514/#laws.0.8.0">1992 c 514 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.7.0">1996 c 398 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/384/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 384 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/33/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 33 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.38.0">2002 c 393 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/8/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2003 c 8 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/78/#laws.0.2.0">2005 c 78 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.2.20.0">2005 c 156 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.66.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 66</a>-69</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.335">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.335 REVIEW OF PLANS AND PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Construction and major remodeling.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner, or any other recipient to whom an appropriation is made to acquire or better public lands or buildings or other public improvements of a capital nature, must not prepare final plans and specifications for any construction, major remodeling, or land acquisition in anticipation of which the appropriation was made until the agency that will use the project has presented the program plan and cost estimates for all elements necessary to complete the project to the chair of the senate Finance Committee and the chair of the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee and the chairs have made their recommendations, and the chair and ranking minority member of the senate Capital Investment Committee and the chair and ranking minority member of the house of representatives Capital Investment Committee are notified. "Construction or major remodeling" means construction of a new building, a substantial addition to an existing building, or a substantial change to the interior configuration of an existing building. The presentation must note any significant changes in the work that will be done, or in its cost, since the appropriation for the project was enacted or from the predesign submittal. The program plans and estimates must be presented for review at least two weeks before a recommendation is needed. The recommendations are advisory only. Failure or refusal to make a recommendation is considered a negative recommendation. The chairs and ranking minority members of the senate Finance and Capital Investment Committees and the house of representatives Capital Investment and Ways and Means Committees must also be notified whenever there is a substantial change in a construction or major remodeling project, or in its cost.</p>
          <p>(b) Capital projects exempt from the requirements of this subdivision include demolition or decommissioning of state assets, hazardous material projects, utility infrastructure projects, environmental testing, parking lots, parking structures, park and ride facilities, bus rapid transit stations, light rail lines, passenger rail projects, freight rail projects, exterior lighting, fencing, highway rest areas, truck stations, storage facilities not consisting primarily of offices or heated work areas, roads, bridges, trails, pathways, campgrounds, athletic fields, dams, floodwater retention systems, water access sites, harbors, sewer separation projects, water and wastewater facilities, port development projects for which the commissioner of transportation has entered into an assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/457A.04">457A.04</a>, ice centers, a local government project with a construction cost of less than $1,500,000, or any other capital project with a construction cost of less than $750,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Other projects.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All other capital projects for which a specific appropriation is made must not proceed until the recipient undertaking the project has notified the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate Capital Investment and Finance Committees and the house of representatives Capital Investment and Ways and Means Committees that the work is ready to begin. Notice is not required for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) capital projects needed to comply with the Americans with Disabilities Act;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) asset preservation projects to which section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.307">16B.307</a> applies;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) projects funded by an agency's operating budget; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) projects funded by a capital asset preservation and replacement account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.632">16A.632</a>, a higher education asset preservation and replacement account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/135A.046">135A.046</a>, or a natural resources asset preservation and replacement account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.946">84.946</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Predesign requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in paragraphs (a) and (b) apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(a) "Predesign" means the stage in the development of a project during which the purpose, scope, cost, and schedule of the complete project are defined and instructions to design professionals are produced.</p>
          <p>(b) "Design" means the stage in the development of a project during which schematic, design development, and contract documents are produced.</p>
          <p>(c) A recipient to whom an appropriation is made for a project subject to review under subdivision 1 or notice under subdivision 2 shall prepare a predesign package and submit it to the commissioner for review and recommendation before proceeding with design activities. The commissioner must complete the review and recommendation within ten working days after receiving it. Failure to review and recommend within the ten days is considered a positive recommendation. The predesign package must be sufficient to define the purpose, scope, cost, and schedule of the project and must demonstrate that the project has been analyzed according to appropriate space needs standards. All predesign, design, and construction projects shall include consideration of the state of Minnesota's correctional industries program, MINNCOR Industries, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.151#stat.16C.151.2">16C.151, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), in predesign planning and product specifications.</p>
          <p>(d) This subdivision does not apply to capital projects for park buildings owned by a local government unit in the metropolitan area defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Energy conservation.</span></h2>
          <p>A recipient to whom a direct appropriation is made for a capital improvement project shall ensure that the project complies with the applicable energy conservation standards contained in law, including sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.19">216C.19</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.20">216C.20</a>, and rules adopted thereunder. The recipient may obtain information and technical assistance from the State Energy Office in the Department of Commerce on energy conservation and alternative energy development relating to the planning and construction of the capital improvement project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Information technology.</span></h2>
          <p>Agency requests for construction and remodeling funds shall include money for cost-effective information technology investments that would enable an agency to reduce its need for office space, provide more of its services electronically, and decentralize its operations. The Department of Information Technology Services must review and approve the information technology portion of construction and major remodeling program plans before the plans are submitted to the chairs of the senate Finance Committee and the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee for their recommendations and the chair and ranking minority member of the senate Capital Investment Committee and the chair and ranking minority member of the house of representatives Capital Investment Committee are notified as required by subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.335.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Information technology review precondition.</span></h2>
          <p>No state agency or department shall propose and the legislature shall not consider building or relocation projects without reviewing implications of utilizing information technology on space utilization.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/300/#laws.1.27.0">1989 c 300 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/591/#laws.6.1.0">1990 c 591 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/610/#laws.1.42.0">1990 c 610 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.4.23.0">1992 c 513 art 4 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/4/#laws.0.11.0">1993 c 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.42.0">1994 c 643 s 42</a>-45; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/2/#laws.1.24.0">1Sp1995 c 2 art 1 s 24</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/463/#laws.0.35.0">1996 c 463 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/159/#laws.2.5.0">1997 c 159 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.3.35.0">1997 c 202 art 3 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/246/#laws.0.11.0">1997 c 246 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/404/#laws.0.34.0">1998 c 404 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.9.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.8.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/12/#laws.0.12.0">1Sp2001 c 12 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/393/#laws.0.39.0">2002 c 393 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.5.23.0">2005 c 156 art 5 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.31.0">2008 c 179 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/365/#laws.0.10.0">2008 c 365 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/370/#laws.0.2.0">2008 c 370 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/189/#laws.0.34.0">2010 c 189 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.30.0">2013 c 134 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.3.36.0">2013 c 142 art 3 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.5.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/227/#laws.1.2.0">2014 c 227 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/294/#laws.2.4.0">2014 c 294 art 2 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/214/#laws.2.5.0">2018 c 214 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.34">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.34 INMATE LABOR.</h1>
        <p>At a state institution or state park or in the maintenance of a state armory, an appropriation for construction, improvements, or maintenance may be expended through the use of inmate or project labor when authorized by the commissioner with the concurrence of the head of the interested state department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.39.0">1984 c 544 s 39</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.35">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.35 ART IN STATE BUILDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Percent of appropriations for art.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An appropriation for the construction or alteration of any state building may contain an amount not to exceed one percent of the total appropriation for the building for the acquisition of works of art, excluding landscaping, which may be an integral part of the building or its grounds, attached to the building or grounds or capable of being displayed in other state buildings. Money used for this purpose is available only for the acquisition of works of art to be exhibited in areas of a building or its grounds accessible, on a regular basis, to members of the public. No more than ten percent of the total amount available each fiscal year under this subdivision may be used for administrative expenses, either by the commissioner of administration or by any other entity to whom the commissioner delegates administrative authority. For the purposes of this section "state building" means a building the construction or alteration of which is paid for wholly or in part by the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Not in prisons.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, no part of a state appropriation may be used to acquire or install works of art in a state correctional facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>A prohibition on using state appropriations to pay for art in correctional facilities does not apply to art produced through programming in correctional facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">PFA excluded.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, an appropriation to the Public Facilities Authority and project financing provided by the authority from the appropriation may not include an amount to acquire works of art.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exempt buildings.</span></h2>
          <p>A building for which the appropriation is less than $500,000 for construction or alteration or a building for which the commissioner of administration has determined that this section is inappropriate is exempt from the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Unused funds.</span></h2>
          <p>If an amount made available under subdivision 1 is not expended for works of art for the building, the unexpended portion is available to the Minnesota Board of the Arts for the commission or purchase of works of art for state buildings existing or for which an appropriation was made prior to June 15, 1983, and is not available to pay construction costs of the building.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Campuses.</span></h2>
          <p>Art for a building on a public college or university campus shall be selected by the campus, in consultation with the Arts Board. Consideration of the artwork of faculty and students on that campus is encouraged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.35.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contractor's bond not required.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/574.26">574.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/574.32">574.32</a> do not apply to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.40.0">1984 c 544 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.11.0">1996 c 398 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.25.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/126/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 126 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/216/#laws.4.1.0">1999 c 216 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.21.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/214/#laws.2.6.0">2018 c 214 art 2 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.355">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.355 COOPERATIVE LOCAL FACILITIES GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grants authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the limits of available appropriations, the commissioner shall make grants to counties, cities, towns, and school districts to acquire, construct, or renovate public land and buildings and other public improvements of a capital nature for cooperative facilities to be owned and operated by the grantees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Match.</span></h2>
          <p>A grant under this section may not be made until the commissioner has determined that at least 30 percent of the total project cost has been committed to the project from nonstate sources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Amount.</span></h2>
          <p>No more than one-third of the amount appropriated by any one appropriation act may be granted to any one project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible to receive a grant, the grant application must be made to the commissioner on behalf of any combination of at least three counties, cities, towns, or school districts. The grant applicants must have entered into a joint powers agreement and formed a joint powers board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> to govern the facilities. The joint powers board must approve the application by resolution.</p>
          <p>(b) The grant application must demonstrate that acquisition, construction, or renovation of the cooperative facilities will improve the delivery of services by the grant applicants and will generate savings to the applicants in operating their buildings and programs.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall prescribe and provide the application form. The application must include at least the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) identification of the facilities;</p>
          <p>(2) a plan for the facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of how the facilities will improve the delivery of governmental services by the applicants;</p>
          <p>(4) a detailed estimate, along with necessary supporting evidence, of the total costs for the facilities;</p>
          <p>(5) an estimate of the dates when the facilities for which the grant is requested will be contracted for and completed;</p>
          <p>(6) a detailed estimate, along with necessary supporting evidence, of the savings in operating costs of buildings and programs that the project will generate;</p>
          <p>(7) the manner in which the applicants will meet the local match requirement; and</p>
          <p>(8) any additional information or material the commissioner prescribes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Priority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of management and budget and the commissioners of other state departments, as appropriate, shall give priority to projects that demonstrate a significant increase in cooperation as measured by one or more of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) improved quality, access, transparency, or level of service to citizens;</p>
          <p>(2) fundamental change in the organization of service delivery;</p>
          <p>(3) substantial savings in operating costs; or</p>
          <p>(4) positive return on investment over the life of the facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.355.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Geographic distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>At least one-half of the money provided as grants each fiscal biennium must be for projects located outside the seven-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.355.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/189/#laws.0.35.0">2010 c 189 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/392/#laws.1.4.0">2010 c 392 art 1 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.356">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.356 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.356.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.356.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Council.</span></h2>
          <p>"Council" means the Minnesota Advisory Council on Infrastructure established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.357">16B.357</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.356.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Infrastructure.</span></h2>
          <p>"Infrastructure" means physical structures and facilities, including but not limited to property, lands, buildings, and other assets of a capital nature. The term includes infrastructure related to agriculture, commerce, communications, economic development, energy, food, health, housing, natural resources, public safety, transportation, drinking water, stormwater, and wastewater.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.356.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.4.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.357">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.357 MINNESOTA ADVISORY COUNCIL ON INFRASTRUCTURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Minnesota Advisory Council on Infrastructure is established as provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the council is to define and maintain a vision for the future of Minnesota's infrastructure that provides for its proper management, coordination, and investment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Voting membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The council consists of the following voting members:</p>
          <p>(1) two members appointed by the governor;</p>
          <p>(2) two members appointed by the senate majority leader;</p>
          <p>(3) two members appointed by the senate minority leader;</p>
          <p>(4) two members appointed by the speaker of the house;</p>
          <p>(5) two members appointed by the house minority leader; and</p>
          <p>(6) one member appointed by the Indian Affairs Council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Nonvoting membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The council consists of the following nonvoting members:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner of administration;</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner of agriculture;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner of commerce;</p>
          <p>(4) the commissioner of employment and economic development;</p>
          <p>(5) the commissioner of health;</p>
          <p>(6) the commissioner of management and budget;</p>
          <p>(7) the commissioner of natural resources;</p>
          <p>(8) the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency;</p>
          <p>(9) the commissioner of transportation;</p>
          <p>(10) the commissioner of Iron Range resources and rehabilitation;</p>
          <p>(11) the chair of the Metropolitan Council;</p>
          <p>(12) the chair of the Board of Water and Soil Resources;</p>
          <p>(13) the executive director of the Minnesota Public Facilities Authority;</p>
          <p>(14) the chancellor of Minnesota State Colleges and Universities; and</p>
          <p>(15) the president of the University of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Voting members; appointment requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An appointing authority under subdivision 2 may only appoint an individual who has direct and practical expertise and experience, whether from the public or private sector, in any of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) asset management in one or more of the areas of planning, design, construction, management, or operations and maintenance, for: (i) drinking water; (ii) wastewater; (iii) stormwater; (iv) transportation; (v) energy; or (vi) communications;</p>
          <p>(2) financial management and procurement; or</p>
          <p>(3) regional asset management across jurisdictions and infrastructure sectors.</p>
          <p>(b) Each appointing authority under subdivision 2, clauses (1) to (5), must appoint one individual who resides in a metropolitan county, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121">473.121, subdivision </a>4, and one individual who resides outside of a metropolitan county.</p>
          <p>(c) No current legislator may be appointed to the council.</p>
          <p>(d) Prior to making appointments, the appointing authorities under subdivision 2 must coordinate and provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) geographic representation throughout the state;</p>
          <p>(2) representation for all major types of infrastructure assets; and</p>
          <p>(3) representation from the public and private sectors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Voting members; recommendations for appointment.</span></h2>
          <p>Each appointing authority under subdivision 2 must acknowledge and give consideration to appointment recommendations made by interested stakeholders, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) the Association of Minnesota Counties;</p>
          <p>(2) the League of Minnesota Cities;</p>
          <p>(3) the Coalition of Greater Minnesota Cities;</p>
          <p>(4) the Minnesota Association of Townships;</p>
          <p>(5) the Minnesota Chapter of the American Public Works Association;</p>
          <p>(6) the Associated General Contractors of Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(7) a labor union representing the building trades;</p>
          <p>(8) a public utility;</p>
          <p>(9) the Minnesota Municipal Utilities Association;</p>
          <p>(10) the Minnesota Chamber of Commerce;</p>
          <p>(11) the Minnesota section of the American Water Works Association;</p>
          <p>(12) the Minnesota Rural Water Association; and</p>
          <p>(13) the Minnesota Rural Electric Association.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Nonvoting members; delegation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.06">15.06</a>, subdivision 6, an individual specified under subdivision 3 may appoint a designee to serve on the council only as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) An individual specified under subdivision 3 may appoint a designee who serves on an ongoing basis to exercise the powers and duties as a nonvoting council member under this section. The designation must be made by written order, filed with the secretary of state. The designee must be a public employee who is:</p>
          <p>(1) a deputy commissioner or deputy director;</p>
          <p>(2) an assistant commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) an immediate subordinate of the appointing authority;</p>
          <p>(4) a director of a relevant office; or</p>
          <p>(5) if the appointing authority is the chair of a board or council specified under subdivision 3, another member of that board or council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Officers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The council must elect from among its voting members a chair, or cochairs, and vice-chair. As necessary, the council may elect other council members to serve as officers.</p>
          <p>(b) The chair is responsible for convening meetings of the council and setting each meeting agenda.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Council actions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A majority of the council, including voting and nonvoting members and excluding vacancies, is a quorum.</p>
          <p>(b) The council may conduct business as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.015">13D.015</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Compensation; terms; removal; vacancies.</span></h2>
          <p>The compensation, membership terms, filling of vacancies, and removal of members on the council are as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Open Meeting Law.</span></h2>
          <p>The council is subject to the Minnesota Open Meeting Law under chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.357.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Data practices.</span></h2>
          <p>The council is subject to the Minnesota Data Practices Act under chapter 13.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.357.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.5.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.358">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.358 POWERS; RESPONSIBILITIES AND DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The council has the nonregulatory powers necessary to carry out its responsibilities and duties specified by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The council is responsible for activities in a nonregulatory capacity and in coordination with stakeholders to identify and recommend best practices that:</p>
          <p>(1) preserve and extend the longevity of Minnesota's public and privately owned infrastructure; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide for effective and efficient management of infrastructure.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless specifically provided otherwise, nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359 </a>requires transfer of personnel, specific responsibilities, or administrative functions from a department or agency to the council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The duties of the council are to:</p>
          <p>(1) identify approaches to enhance and expedite infrastructure coordination across jurisdictions, agencies, state and local government, and public and private sectors, including in planning, design, engineering, construction, maintenance, and operations;</p>
          <p>(2) analyze methods to improve efficiency and the use of resources related to (i) public infrastructure, and (ii) public asset management practices;</p>
          <p>(3) identify opportunities to reduce duplication in infrastructure projects and asset management;</p>
          <p>(4) identify barriers and gaps in effective asset management;</p>
          <p>(5) identify objectives and strategies that enhance the longevity and adaptability of infrastructure throughout the state;</p>
          <p>(6) develop advisory recommendations, if any, related to the responsibilities and duties specified under this section, including to state agencies for programs, policies, and practices; and</p>
          <p>(7) implement the requirements under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Asset managers program.</span></h2>
          <p>The council must develop and recommend a plan for a statewide asset managers program that provides for:</p>
          <p>(1) identification, exchange, and distribution of (i) information on existing asset management tools and resources, and (ii) best practices on infrastructure management;</p>
          <p>(2) training for infrastructure owners and asset managers; and</p>
          <p>(3) coordination and collaboration among infrastructure owners and asset managers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Administrative support.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must provide the council with suitable space to maintain an office, hold meetings, and keep records. The commissioner must provide administrative staff and information technology resources to the council as necessary for the expeditious conduct of the council's duties and responsibilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.358.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By December 15 annually, the council must submit a report to the governor and the legislative committees with jurisdiction over capital investment, climate, economic development, energy, and transportation. At a minimum, the report must:</p>
          <p>(1) summarize the activities of the council;</p>
          <p>(2) provide an overview for each of the duties and requirements under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) identify any barriers and constraints related to activities of the council; and</p>
          <p>(4) provide any recommendations of the council.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.358.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.6.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.359">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.359 PERSONNEL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.359.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Executive director.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must hire an executive director in the classified service, with the advice of the council. The executive director is the principal administrative officer for the council. The executive director is not an ex officio member of the council.</p>
          <p>(b) The executive director must have (1) leadership or management experience, and (2) training and experience in public works or asset management.</p>
          <p>(c) The executive director must perform the duties as specified by the council to manage and implement the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.356">16B.356</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.359">16B.359</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.359.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Staffing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The executive director must:</p>
          <p>(1) hire any employees on the basis of merit and fitness that the executive director considers necessary to discharge the functions of the office; and</p>
          <p>(2) prescribe the powers and duties of an employee.</p>
          <p>(b) The executive director may:</p>
          <p>(1) hire a deputy director and other staff; and</p>
          <p>(2) delegate the powers, duties, and responsibilities of the executive director to employees, under conditions prescribed by the executive director.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.359.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.7.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SERVICES TO STATE AGENCIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.36">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.36 INVESTIGATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.36.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may examine, investigate, or make a survey of the organization, administration, and management of state agencies and institutions under their control, and may assist state agencies by providing analytical, statistical, and organizational development services to them in order to secure greater efficiency and economy through reorganization or consolidation of agencies or functions and to eliminate duplication of function, effort, or activity, so far as possible. The commissioner shall periodically submit to the legislature a list of the studies being conducted for this purpose and any future studies scheduled at the time the list is submitted. For purposes of this section, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities is a state agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.36.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall recommend to the legislature any necessary changes in the laws of the state as a result of a survey or investigation, or otherwise, in order to secure a better organization of the state government or greater efficiency and economy in administration. For this purpose, the commissioner may hold hearings, and issue subpoenas for and compel the attendance of witnesses, the giving of testimony, and the production of books, records, accounts, documents, and papers, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.08">15.08</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.41.0">1984 c 544 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.123.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.61.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 398 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.361">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.361 OFFICE OF COLLABORATION AND DISPUTE RESOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.361.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties of the office.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of administration shall maintain the Office of Collaboration and Dispute Resolution within the Department of Administration. The office must:</p>
          <p>(1) assist state agencies; offices of the executive, legislative, and judicial branches; Tribal governments; and units of local government in improving collaboration, dispute resolution, and public engagement;</p>
          <p>(2) promote and utilize collaborative dispute resolution models and processes based on documented best practices to foster trust, relationships, mutual understanding, consensus-based resolutions, and wise and durable solutions, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(i) using established criteria and procedures for identifying and assessing collaborative dispute resolution projects;</p>
          <p>(ii) designing collaborative dispute resolution processes;</p>
          <p>(iii) preparing and training participants; and</p>
          <p>(iv) facilitating meetings and group processes using collaborative techniques and approaches;</p>
          <p>(3) support collaboration and dispute resolution in the public and private sectors by providing technical assistance and information on best practices and new developments in dispute resolution fields;</p>
          <p>(4) build capacity and educate the public and government entities on collaboration, dispute resolution approaches, and public engagement;</p>
          <p>(5) promote the broad use of community mediation in the state; and</p>
          <p>(6) ensure that all areas of the state have access to services by providing grants to private nonprofit entities certified by the state court administrator under chapter 494 that assist in resolution of disputes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.361.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Awarding grants to assist in resolution of disputes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall, to the extent funds are appropriated for this purpose, make grants to private nonprofit community mediation entities certified by the state court administrator under chapter 494 that assist in resolution of disputes under subdivision 1, clause (6). The commissioner shall establish a grant review committee to assist in the review of grant applications and the allocation of grants under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) To be eligible for a grant under this section, a nonprofit organization must meet the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/494.05#stat.494.05.1">494.05, subdivision 1</a>, clauses (1), (2), (4), and (5).</p>
          <p>(c) A nonprofit entity receiving a grant must agree to comply with guidelines adopted by the state court administrator under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/494.015#stat.494.015.1">494.015, subdivision 1</a>. Policies adopted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a> apply to grants under this section. The exclusions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/494.03">494.03</a> apply to grants under this section.</p>
          <p>(d) Grantees must report data required under chapter 494 to evaluate quality and outcomes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.361.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Accepting funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may apply for and receive money made available from federal, state, or other sources for the purposes of carrying out the mission of the Office of Collaboration and Dispute Resolution. Funds received under this subdivision are appropriated to the commissioner for their intended purpose.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.361.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.70.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 70</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.37">
