        <h1 class="shn">16B.37 REORGANIZATION OF AGENCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's authority.</span></h2>
          <p>To improve efficiency and avoid duplication, the commissioner may transfer personnel, powers, or duties, or any combination of them, from a state agency to another state agency that has been in existence for at least one year prior to the date of transfer. A transfer must have received the prior approval of the governor. The commissioner shall no later than January 15 of each year submit to the legislature a bill making all statutory changes required by reorganization orders issued by the commissioner during the preceding calendar year. For purposes of this section, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities is a state agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reorganization order.</span></h2>
          <p>A transfer made pursuant to subdivision 1 must be in the form of a reorganization order. A proposed reorganization order must be submitted to the chairs of the governmental operations committees in the house of representatives and the senate at least 30 days before being filed with the secretary of state. A reorganization order must be filed with the secretary of state, be uniform in format, and be numbered consecutively. An order is effective upon filing with the secretary of state and remains in effect until amended or superseded. Copies of the filed order must be delivered promptly by the commissioner to the secretary of the senate, the chief clerk of the house, and the chairs of the governmental operations committees in the senate and house of representatives. A reorganization order which transfers all or substantially all of the powers or duties or personnel of a department, the Housing Finance Agency, or the Pollution Control Agency is not effective until it is ratified by concurrent resolution or enacted into law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.37.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall determine the fractional part of the appropriation to the transferor agency that is represented by the transferred personnel, power, or duty, and that part of the appropriation is reappropriated to the transferee agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.37.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Work of department for another.</span></h2>
          <p>To avoid duplication and improve efficiency, the commissioner may direct an agency to do work for another agency or may direct a division or section of an agency to do work for another division or section within the same agency and shall require reimbursement for the work. Reimbursements received by an agency are reappropriated to the account making the original expenditure in accordance with the transfer procedure established by the commissioner of management and budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.37.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Employees assigned.</span></h2>
          <p>With the approval of the governor and by agreement of the heads of the departments or agencies concerned, any appointive subordinate officer or employee of a department or agency may be employed by or assigned to perform duties under another department or agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.42.0">1984 c 544 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/262/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 262 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.13.0">1996 c 398 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.371">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.371 ASSISTANCE TO SMALL AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner may provide administrative support services to small agencies. To promote efficiency and cost-effective use of state resources, and to improve financial controls, the commissioner may require a small agency to receive administrative support services through the Department of Administration or through another agency designated by the commissioner. Services subject to this section include finance, accounting, payroll, purchasing, human resources, and other services designated by the commissioner. The commissioner may determine what constitutes a small agency for purposes of this section. The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of management and budget and small agencies, shall evaluate small agencies' needs for administrative support services. If the commissioner provides administrative support services to a small agency, the commissioner must enter into a service level agreement with the agency, specifying the services to be provided and the costs and anticipated outcomes of the services.</p>
        <p>(b) The Minnesota Council on Latino Affairs, the Council for Minnesotans of African Heritage, the Council on Asian-Pacific Minnesotans, the Indian Affairs Council, and the Minnesota State Council on Disability must use the services specified in paragraph (a).</p>
        <p class="in">(c) The commissioner of administration may assess agencies for services it provides under this section. The amounts assessed are appropriated to the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(d) For agencies covered in this section, the commissioner has the authority to require the agency to comply with applicable state finance, accounting, payroll, purchasing, and human resources policies. The agencies served retain the ownership and responsibility for spending decisions and for ongoing implementation of appropriate business operations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.371.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/278/#laws.2.7.0">2012 c 278 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.87.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 87</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.372">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.372 ENVIRONMENTAL SUSTAINABILITY GOVERNMENT OPERATIONS; OFFICE CREATED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.372.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Enterprise sustainability.</span></h2>
          <p>The Office of Enterprise Sustainability is established to assist all state agencies in making measurable progress toward improving the sustainability of government operations by reducing the impact on the environment, controlling unnecessary waste of natural resources and public funds, and spurring innovation. The office shall create new tools and share best practices, assist state agencies to plan for and implement improvements, and monitor progress toward achieving intended outcomes. Specific duties include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) managing a sustainability metrics and reporting system, including a public dashboard that allows Minnesotans to track progress and is updated annually;</p>
          <p>(2) assisting agencies in developing and executing sustainability plans; and</p>
          <p>(3) implementing the state building energy conservation improvement revolving loan in Minnesota Statutes, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.86">16B.86</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.87">16B.87</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.372.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State agency responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>Each cabinet-level agency is required to participate in the sustainability effort by developing a sustainability plan and by making measurable progress toward improving associated sustainability outcomes. State agencies and boards that are not members of the cabinet shall take steps toward improving sustainability outcomes. However, they are not required to participate at the level of cabinet-level agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.372.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local governments.</span></h2>
          <p>The Office of Enterprise Sustainability shall make reasonable attempts to share tools and best practices with local governments.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.372.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.71.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 71</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.373">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.373 OFFICE OF ENTERPRISE TRANSLATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.373.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Office establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish an Office of Enterprise Translations. The office must:</p>
          <p>(1) provide translation services for written material for executive agencies;</p>
          <p>(2) create and maintain language-specific landing webpages in Spanish, Hmong, and Somali and other languages that may be determined by the commissioner, in consultation with the state demographer, with links to translated materials at state agency websites; and</p>
          <p>(3) serve as a resource to executive agencies in areas such as best practices and standards for the translation of written materials.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall determine the process and requirements for state agencies to request translations of written materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.373.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Language access service account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The language access service account is created in the special revenue fund for reimbursing state agencies for expenses incurred in providing language translation services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.373.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.72.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 72</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.38">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.38 DISSOLVED OR SUSPENDED AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall undertake all necessary administrative functions of an agency which has been temporarily or permanently dissolved or suspended. These functions may include but are not limited to: authorizing payment of all obligations of the dissolved or suspended agency including payroll certifications; serving as custodian for and disposing of all property of the agency; and, in the event that the agency is only temporarily dissolved or suspended, serving as its chief administrative officer with all necessary powers until the agency is reconstituted. To implement these responsibilities the commissioner may spend any necessary money from a dissolved or suspended agency's appropriation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.43.0">1984 c 544 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.39">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.39 PROGRAMS FOR STATE EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.25.0">1987 c 365 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.39.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Endowment fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of administration may establish an endowment fund to reward state agencies and their employees for improving productivity and service quality. The commissioner shall use gift money to establish the fund. The interest earnings are appropriated to the commissioner to make agency and employee awards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.319">43A.319</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/531/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 531 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.44.0">1984 c 544 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.11.0">1987 c 365 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/343/#laws.0.5.0">1989 c 343 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/337/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 337 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.114.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 114</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.405"><b>16B.405</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.15">16E.15</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.41"><b>16B.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.3.36.0">1997 c 202 art 3 s 36</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.43"><b>16B.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.3.36.0">1997 c 202 art 3 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.44"><b>16B.44</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.16">16E.16</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.45">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.45 FUNCTION OF LEGISLATIVE AUDITOR.</h1>
        <p>The legislative auditor may conduct performance evaluations of all systems analysis, information services, and computerization efforts of agencies, the University of Minnesota, and metropolitan boards, agencies, and commissions. Upon request of the governing body or the state Information Systems Advisory Council, the legislative auditor shall conduct the same services for political subdivisions of the state and report the findings to the governor and the legislature. The cost of these evaluations must be paid by the agencies being evaluated.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.50.0">1984 c 544 s 50</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.46"><b>16B.46</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.17">16E.17</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.467"><b>16B.467</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.20">16E.20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.47"><b>16B.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.6.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.48">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.48 GENERAL SERVICES REVOLVING FUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.48.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reimbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees prescribed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.51">16B.51</a>, for the rendering of the services provided in that section are deposited in the state treasury by the collecting agency and credited to the general services revolving fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.48.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the state treasury credited to the general services revolving fund and money that is deposited in the fund is appropriated annually to the commissioner for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) to operate the central mailing service, including purchasing postage and related items and refunding postage deposits;</p>
          <p>(2) to operate a documents service as prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.51">16B.51</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) to provide services for the maintenance, operation, and upkeep of buildings and grounds managed by the commissioner of administration;</p>
          <p>(4) to provide analytical, statistical, and organizational development services to state agencies, local units of government, metropolitan and regional agencies, and school districts; and</p>
          <p>(5) to perform services for any other agency. Money may be expended for this purpose only when directed by the governor. The agency receiving the services shall reimburse the fund for their cost, and the commissioner shall make the appropriate transfers when requested. The term "services" as used in this clause means compensation paid officers and employees of the state government; supplies, materials, equipment, and other articles and things used by or furnished to an agency; and utility services and other services for the maintenance, operation, and upkeep of buildings and offices of the state government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.48.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.5.24.0">2005 c 156 art 5 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.48.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reimbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as specifically provided otherwise by law, each agency shall reimburse the general services revolving funds for the cost of all services, supplies, materials, labor, and depreciation of equipment, including reasonable overhead costs, which the commissioner is authorized and directed to furnish an agency. The cost of all publications or other materials produced by the commissioner and financed from the general services revolving fund must include reasonable overhead costs. The commissioner of administration shall report the rates to be charged for the general services revolving funds no later than July 1 each year to the chair of the committee or division in the senate and house of representatives with primary jurisdiction over the budget of the Department of Administration. The commissioner of management and budget shall make appropriate transfers to the revolving funds described in this section when requested by the commissioner of administration. The commissioner of administration may make allotments, encumbrances, and, with the approval of the commissioner of management and budget, disbursements in anticipation of such transfers. In addition, the commissioner of administration, with the approval of the commissioner of management and budget, may require an agency to make advance payments to the revolving funds in this section sufficient to cover the agency's estimated obligation for a period of at least 60 days. All reimbursements and other money received by the commissioner of administration under this section must be deposited in the appropriate revolving fund. Any earnings remaining in the fund established to account for the documents service prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.51">16B.51</a> at the end of each fiscal year not otherwise needed for present or future operations, as determined by the commissioners of administration and management and budget, must be transferred to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.48.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Liquidation.</span></h2>
          <p>If the general services revolving funds are abolished or liquidated, the total net profit from the operation of each fund must be distributed to the various funds from which purchases were made. The amount to be distributed to each fund must bear to the net profit the same ratio as the total purchases from each fund bears to the total purchases from all the funds during the same period of time.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.53.0">1984 c 544 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.50.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.125.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/363/#laws.0.7.0">1986 c 363 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/613/#laws.0.13.0">1988 c 613 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.10.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/506/#laws.2.13.0">1990 c 506 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.65.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.10.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/457/#laws.0.8.0">1996 c 457 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.12.15.0">2000 c 488 art 12 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.44.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.5.4.0">2005 c 156 art 5 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/215/#laws.12.26.0">2010 c 215 art 12 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.4.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.13.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.4805">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.4805 ACCOMMODATION REIMBURSEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>"Reasonable accommodation" as used in this section has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.08">363A.08</a>. "State agency" as used in this section has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.011#stat.16A.011.12">16A.011, subdivision 12</a>. "Reasonable accommodations eligible for reimbursement" means:</p>
          <p>(1) reasonable accommodations provided to applicants for employment;</p>
          <p>(2) reasonable accommodations for employees for services that will need to be provided on a periodic or ongoing basis; or</p>
          <p>(3) reasonable accommodations that involve onetime expenses that total more than $500 for an employee in a fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement for making reasonable accommodation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of administration shall reimburse state agencies for up to 50 percent of the cost of expenses incurred in making reasonable accommodations eligible for reimbursement for agency employees and applicants for employment to the extent that funds are available in the accommodation account established under subdivision 3 for this purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Accommodation account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The accommodation account is created as an account in the special revenue fund for reimbursing state agencies for expenses incurred in providing reasonable accommodation eligible for reimbursement for agency employees and applicants for agency employment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may use up to 15 percent of the biennial appropriation for administration of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>By August 1, 2015, or within 30 days of final enactment, whichever is later, and each year thereafter by June 30, the commissioner of administration must notify state agencies that reimbursement for expenses incurred to make reasonable accommodation eligible for reimbursement for agency employees and applicants for agency employment is available under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4805.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 31 of each year, the commissioner of administration must report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the house of representatives and the senate committees with jurisdiction over state government finance on the use of the central accommodation account during the prior calendar year. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the number and type of accommodations requested;</p>
          <p>(2) the cost of accommodations requested;</p>
          <p>(3) the state agencies from which the requests were made;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of requests made for employees and the number of requests for applicants for employment;</p>
          <p>(5) the number and type of accommodations that were not provided;</p>
          <p>(6) any remaining balance left in the account;</p>
          <p>(7) if the account was depleted, the date on which funds were exhausted and the number, type, and cost of accommodations that were not reimbursed to state agencies; and</p>
          <p>(8) a description of how the account was promoted to state agencies.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.4805.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.8.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.73.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 73</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.481">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.481 FEES FOR TRAINING AND MAINTENANCE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may charge state agencies and political subdivisions a fee for the cost of energy conservation training and preventive maintenance programs. Fees collected by the commissioner must be deposited in the state treasury and are appropriated to the commissioner to pay the cost of the training and maintenance programs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.481.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.13.0">1987 c 365 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.482"><b>16B.482</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.1.35.0">1998 c 386 art 1 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.4821">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.4821 PROVISION OF MATERIALS AND SERVICES TO MNSCU.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Materials and services available.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities may request from the commissioner of administration any services and materials available to any state agency under this chapter, including but not limited to purchasing, contracting, leasing, energy conservation, communications systems, construction, and all other programs and contracts administered by the Department of Administration, whether administered directly or indirectly by contract or otherwise. The commissioner of administration shall make reasonable efforts to comply with any such request. The chancellor of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities and the commissioner of administration shall cooperate to identify services and materials available to state agencies from the Department of Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Status requested by chancellor.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota State Colleges and Universities shall be a state agency where being a state agency is a prerequisite to obtaining or participating in any services, materials acquisition, or programs under this chapter which are requested by the chancellor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.4821.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota State Colleges and Universities shall be a state agency for purposes of being included on any state agency's list to receive notices and information appropriate to the purposes of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.4821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.17.0">1996 c 398 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.483"><b>16B.483</b> MS 2012 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05">16C.05, subd 2</a>, para (f)]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.485">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.485 INTERFUND LOANS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may, with the approval of the commissioner of management and budget, make loans from an internal service or enterprise fund to another internal service or enterprise fund, and the amount necessary is appropriated from the fund that makes the loan. The commissioner shall report the amount and purpose of the loan to the chair of the committee or division in the senate and house of representatives with primary jurisdiction over the budget of the Department of Administration. The term of a loan made under this section must be not more than 24 months.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.485.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.59.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.12.16.0">2000 c 488 art 12 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.49">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.49 CENTRAL MAILING SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall maintain and operate for state agencies, departments, institutions, and offices a central mail handling unit. Official, outgoing mail for units in St. Paul must be delivered unstamped to the unit. The unit shall also operate an interoffice mail distribution system. The department may add personnel and acquire equipment that may be necessary to operate the unit efficiently and cost-effectively. Account must be kept of the postage required on that mail, which is then a proper charge against the agency delivering the mail. To provide funds for the payment of postage, each agency shall make advance payments to the commissioner sufficient to cover its postage obligations for at least 60 days. For purposes of this section, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities is a state agency.</p>
        <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a) or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.09">16C.09</a>, the commissioner may approve the performance of mail-related functions by an agency outside the state's central mail-handling unit if the agency demonstrates it furthers program effectiveness, better use of services, greater efficiency, or greater economy in state government.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.54.0">1984 c 544 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.13.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.18.0">1996 c 398 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/206/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 206 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/6/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2005 c 6 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.495">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.495 HAZARD IDENTIFICATION AND ACCIDENT PREVENTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner must operate a program of occupational hazard identification and accident prevention for state agencies and state employees, and shall provide the staff, equipment, and facilities needed for the program. The program must be offered to all state agencies through the agency safety contact or other designee; is consultative in nature; and must assist state agencies with the goal of providing a safe work environment, safe work methods, and hazard identification.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must cooperate with the Department of Labor and Industry and the Department of Health, as well as other private and public community agencies, to assist in the objective of hazard identification and accident prevention.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.495.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/97/#laws.0.5.0">1997 c 97 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.10.0">2008 c 204 s 10,42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.50"><b>16B.50</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.136.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 136</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.51">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.51 AGENCY REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Supervision by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall supervise and control the making and distribution of all reports and other publications of all kinds issued by the state and state agencies when not otherwise prescribed by law. The commissioner shall also prescribe the manner and form of issuing reports required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.08">8.08</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.50">16A.50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.03">35.03</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/129D.02#stat.129D.02.5">129D.02, subdivision 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01">256.01</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299C.18">299C.18</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prescribe fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may prescribe fees to be charged for services rendered by the state or an agency in furnishing to those who request them certified copies of records or other documents, certifying that records or documents do not exist and furnishing other reports, publications, data, or related material which is requested. The fees, unless otherwise prescribed by law, may be fixed at the market rate. The commissioner of management and budget is authorized to approve the prescribed rates for the purpose of assuring that they, in total, will result in receipts greater than costs in the fund. Fees prescribed under this subdivision are deposited in the state treasury by the collecting agency and credited to the general services revolving fund. Nothing in this subdivision permits the commissioner of administration to furnish any service which is now prohibited or unauthorized by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.51.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sale of publications.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may sell official reports, documents, data, and publications of all kinds, may delegate their sale to state agencies, and may establish facilities for their sale within the Department of Administration and elsewhere within the state service. The commissioner may remit a portion of the price of any publication or data to the agency producing the publication or data. Money that is remitted to an agency is annually appropriated to that agency to discharge the costs of preparing the publications or data.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.51.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to the Regents of the University of Minnesota or to the State Agricultural Society.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.51.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Limitations on subject matter prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may not adopt rules to prescribe the fees permitted by subdivision 2 or which limit in any way the subject matter of a report or publication which the law requires or authorizes an agency to produce.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.56.0">1984 c 544 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.45.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 45</a>,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.14.0">1987 c 365 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/394/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 394 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.14.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.6.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/227/#laws.2.2.0">2014 c 227 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.52"><b>16B.52</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.2.52.0">2005 c 156 art 2 s 52</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.53">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.53 SALE OF LAWS AND RESOLUTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide for the sale and distribution of copies of laws and resolutions on file in the Office of the Secretary of State in accordance with this section. The secretary of state shall cooperate with the commissioner in furnishing the services provided for in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Charges.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish charges for those laws and resolutions sufficient to cover their cost. Fees established for the sale and distribution of laws and resolutions, including mailing and postage charges, may be accepted by the commissioner in advance, and any unused portions amounting to $1 or more may be returned to the person entitled to them upon request, notwithstanding the provision of any other law prohibiting refunds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.53.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revolving fund.</span></h2>
          <p>Money collected by the commissioner under this section must be deposited in the general services revolving fund in the state treasury. Money in that fund is annually appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of carrying out this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/480/#laws.0.15.0">1984 c 480 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.58.0">1984 c 544 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.1.9.0">1990 c 426 art 1 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TRAVEL MANAGEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.531">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.531 TRAVEL SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may offer a centralized travel service to all state departments and agencies, and to the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, and may, in connection with that service, accept payments from travel agencies under contracts for the provision of travel services. The payments must be deposited in the motor pool revolving account established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.54#stat.16B.54.8">16B.54, subdivision 8</a>, and must be used for the expenses of managing the centralized travel service. Revenues in excess of the management costs of the centralized service must be returned to the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.531.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 365 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.19.0">1996 c 398 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.54">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.54 ENTERPRISE FLEET; ESTABLISHMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Motor pools.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall manage an enterprise fleet of passenger motor vehicles and trucks used by state agencies with principal offices in the city of St. Paul and may provide for branch enterprise fleets at other places within the state. For purposes of this section, (1) "agencies" includes the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, (2) "passenger motor vehicle" means an on-road vehicle capable of transporting people, and (3) "truck" means a pickup or panel truck up to one ton carrying capacity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may direct an agency to make a transfer of a passenger motor vehicle or truck currently assigned to it. The transfer must be made to the commissioner for use in the enterprise fleet. The commissioner shall reimburse an agency whose motor vehicles have been paid for with funds dedicated by the constitution for a special purpose and which are assigned to the enterprise fleet. The amount of reimbursement for a motor vehicle is its average wholesale price as determined from the midwest edition of the National Automobile Dealers Association official used car guide.</p>
          <p>(b) To the extent that funds are available for the purpose, the commissioner may purchase or otherwise acquire additional passenger motor vehicles and trucks necessary for the enterprise fleet. The title to all motor vehicles assigned to or purchased or acquired for the enterprise fleet is in the name of the Department of Administration.</p>
          <p>(c) On the request of an agency, the commissioner may transfer to the enterprise fleet any passenger motor vehicle or truck for the purpose of disposing of it. The department or agency transferring the vehicle or truck must be paid for it from the motor pool revolving account established by this section in an amount equal to two-thirds of the average wholesale price of the vehicle or truck as determined from the midwest edition of the National Automobile Dealers Association official used car guide.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall provide for the uniform marking of all motor vehicles. Motor vehicle colors must be selected from the regular color chart provided by the manufacturer each year. The commissioner may further provide for the use of motor vehicles without marking by:</p>
          <p>(1) the governor;</p>
          <p>(2) the lieutenant governor;</p>
          <p>(3) the Division of Criminal Apprehension, the Division of Alcohol and Gambling Enforcement, and arson investigators of the Division of Fire Marshal in the Department of Public Safety;</p>
          <p>(4) the Financial Institutions Division and investigative staff of the Department of Commerce;</p>
          <p>(5) the Division of Disease Prevention and Control of the Department of Health;</p>
          <p>(6) the State Lottery;</p>
          <p>(7) criminal investigators of the Department of Revenue;</p>
          <p>(8) state-owned community service facilities in Direct Care and Treatment;</p>
          <p>(9) the Office of the Attorney General;</p>
          <p>(10) the investigative staff of the Gambling Control Board; and</p>
          <p>(11) the Department of Corrections inmate community work crew program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.91#stat.352.91.3g">352.91, subdivision 3g</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Responsible person; personnel.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner is responsible for the control, regulation, acquisition, operation, maintenance, repair, and disposal of all motor vehicles of the enterprise fleet. The commissioner may employ a director and other necessary classified employees for the operation of the enterprise fleet in accordance with chapter 43A.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Maintenance, repair, and storage; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) <b>Maintenance, repair, storage.</b> The commissioner may contract with the head of an agency or another person operating facilities for the maintenance, repair, and storage of motor vehicles to provide for maintenance, repair, and storage of motor vehicles of the enterprise fleet.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Appropriation.</b> Money received by the head of an agency under a contract with the commissioner under this subdivision is annually appropriated to the agency for the same purposes as money expended by the agency head for the operation of state-owned facilities for the maintenance, repair, and storage of motor pool vehicles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Use of motor vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>The motor vehicles in the enterprise fleet are for official state business only. An agency requiring the services of a motor vehicle shall request it from the enterprise fleet on either a temporary or permanent basis. No privately owned motor vehicle may be used for official state business except when authorized by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Schedule of charges.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency using the facilities of the enterprise fleet shall periodically reimburse the commissioner for the services, in accordance with the schedule of charges the commissioner establishes. This schedule of charges must be based on the costs incurred in operating the enterprise fleet, including reasonable overhead costs, vehicle depreciation, insurance for public liability and property damage, and other costs. The commissioner must retain records and reports and all schedules used as a basis for charging state agencies for the services furnished.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to motor vehicles of the State Patrol or the University of Minnesota, or to motor vehicles of any other agency which are specially equipped for the needs of that agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.54.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Motor pool revolving account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) <b>Account established.</b> Money or reimbursements the commissioner receives from the operation of the enterprise fleet is deposited in the state treasury and credited to a motor pool revolving account. Money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner to carry out this section. The motor pool revolving account may be used to provide material transfer services to agencies.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Unobligated excess transferred.</b> When the unobligated amount of money in the state treasury credited to the motor pool revolving account exceeds the average monthly operating expense at the end of the fiscal year, the unobligated amount in excess of one month's operating expense must be transferred to the general fund in the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.59.0">1984 c 544 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.126.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 126</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/277/#laws.1.1.0">1989 c 277 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/334/#laws.6.4.0">1989 c 334 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/506/#laws.2.14.0">1990 c 506 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/572/#laws.0.8.0">1990 c 572 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/233/#laws.0.109.0">1991 c 233 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/486/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 486 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/269/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 269 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.20.0">1996 c 398 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/129/#laws.2.1.0">1997 c 129 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/206/#laws.0.6.0">1997 c 206 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/8/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2001 c 8 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.2.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.1.14.0">2021 c 31 art 1 s 14</a>,15,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.42.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.42.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.55">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.55 USE OF STATE VEHICLES; COMPENSATION FOR USE OF PERSONAL VEHICLES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "state vehicle" means a vehicle owned or leased by the state or loaned to the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibited uses.</span></h2>
          <p>A state vehicle may be used only for authorized state business. A state vehicle may not be used for transportation to or from the residence of a state employee, except as provided in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permitted uses.</span></h2>
          <p>A state vehicle may be used by a state employee to travel to or from the employee's residence:</p>
          <p>(1) on a day on which it may be necessary for the employee to respond to a work-related emergency during hours when the employee is not normally working;</p>
          <p>(2) if the employee has been assigned the use of a state vehicle for authorized state business on an extended basis, and the employee's primary place of work is not the state work station to which the employee is permanently assigned;</p>
          <p>(3) if the employee has been assigned the use of a state vehicle for authorized state business away from the work station to which the employee is permanently assigned, and the number of miles traveled, or the time needed to conduct the business, will be minimized if the employee uses a state vehicle to travel to the employee's residence before or after traveling to the place of state business; or</p>
          <p>(4) if the employee is authorized to participate in a ride-sharing program established by the commissioner pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.257">174.257</a>.</p>
          <p>Use of a state vehicle under this subdivision requires the prior approval of the agency head or the designee of the agency head.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Personal vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>No state employee shall be compensated by the state for use of a personal vehicle for travel between the employee's residence and the state work station to which the employee is permanently assigned, except pursuant to a collective bargaining agreement negotiated under chapter 179A or a compensation plan adopted by the commissioner of management and budget under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.05">43A.05</a>. A collective bargaining agreement or compensation plan may only provide for this compensation in cases in which an employee is called back to work during hours when the employee is not normally working.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exclusions.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivisions 2 to 4 do not apply to the van pooling program established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.56">16B.56</a>, to a ride-sharing program established by the Department of Transportation, to a trooper employed by the State Patrol, or to use of a state vehicle by the governor or lieutenant governor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.55.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Vehicle operating procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall set operating procedures for use of state vehicles. These operating procedures are not subject to the Administrative Procedure Act.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.60.0">1984 c 544 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/613/#laws.0.14.0">1988 c 613 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 7 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.42.0">2008 c 204 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.56">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.56 COMMUTER VANS; USE BY STATE EMPLOYEES AND SPOUSES AND OTHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Employee transportation program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) <b>Establishment.</b> To conserve energy and alleviate traffic congestion around state offices, the commissioner shall, in cooperation with the commissioner of transportation, the State Energy Office in the Department of Commerce, and interested nonprofit agencies, establish and operate an employee transportation program using commuter vans with a capacity of not less than seven nor more than 16 passengers. Commuter vans may be used by state employees and others to travel between their homes and their work locations. However, only state employee drivers may use the van for personal purposes after working hours, not including partisan political activity. The commissioner shall acquire or lease commuter vans, or otherwise contract for the provision of commuter vans, and shall make the vans available for the use of state employees and others in accordance with standards and procedures adopted by the commissioner. The commissioner shall promote the maximum participation of state employees and others in the use of the vans.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Administrative policies.</b> The commissioner shall adopt standards and procedures under this section without regard to chapter 14. The commissioner shall provide for the recovery by the state of vehicle acquisition, lease, operation, and insurance costs through efficient and convenient assignment of vans, and for the billing of costs and collection of fees. A state employee using a van for personal use shall pay, pursuant to the standards and procedures adopted by the commissioner, for operating and routine maintenance costs incurred as a result of the personal use. Fees collected under this subdivision shall be deposited in the accounts from which the costs of operating, maintaining, and leasing or amortization for the specific vehicle are paid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible participants.</span></h2>
          <p>State employees and their spouses and other people are eligible for the employee transportation program established by this section, if the driver and substitute driver of every vanpool are state employees and if state employees constitute a majority of the members of every vanpool. Available space in vanpools must, whenever possible, be filled by state employees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Areas of use.</span></h2>
          <p>Use of the vans pursuant to this section is limited to areas not having adequate public transportation between the residences of state employees and others and their places of employment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.26.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Insurance; limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.31">15.31</a> or any other law to the contrary, the commissioner may purchase, pursuant to this chapter, collision insurance coverage for the commuter vans. Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.54#stat.16B.54.2">16B.54, subdivision 2</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.012">168.012</a>, the vans may not be marked. The vans may not be equipped with tax-exempt motor vehicle number plates.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.56.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/408/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 408 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/408/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 408 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.61.0">1984 c 544 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.10.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 10</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.9.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.57">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.57 GASOLINE AND PETROLEUM PRODUCTS, SOURCE OF SUPPLY FOR AGENCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Petroleum products facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may require a state agency which has facilities for the storage and distribution of gasoline and other petroleum products to furnish gasoline and other petroleum products to any other state agency and shall require payment to compensate for the cost of those products. The commissioner shall prescribe all procedures for the guidance of state agencies in carrying out the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>Money paid by one state agency to another to compensate for the cost of products furnished under subdivision 1 is annually appropriated to the state agency which furnishes those products.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.57.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.62.0">1984 c 544 s 62</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.58">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.58 STATE PARKING FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Powers and duties of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>No person may park a motor vehicle, either privately or publicly owned, upon any parking lot or facility owned or operated by the state except as authorized by this section. The commissioner shall operate and supervise all state parking lots and facilities associated with buildings described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.24#stat.16B.24.1">16B.24, subdivision 1</a>, or when the commissioner considers it advisable and practicable, any other parking lots or facilities owned or rented by the state for the use of a state agency or state employees. The commissioner may also provide employee shuttle service and promote alternative transportation modes, including initiatives to increase the number of multi-occupancy vehicles. The commissioner may fix and collect rents, charges, or fees in connection with and for the use of any state parking lot or facility within the cities of St. Paul and Minneapolis except for any state lot or facility the control of which is vested by law in a state agency other than the Department of Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>Copies of the commissioner's rules under this section must be provided to all contract parkers. Each parking lot or facility must be posted with notice of who is entitled to park there.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Removal and impounding of vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>A motor vehicle parked on a state parking lot or facility in violation of the rules of the commissioner is a public nuisance and the commissioner shall provide for the abatement of the nuisance by rules, including provision for the removal and impounding of the motor vehicle. The cost of the removal and impounding is a lien against the motor vehicle until paid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Violations.</span></h2>
          <p>A person, elective or appointed state official, firm, association, or corporation which violates any of the provisions of this section or any rule made by the commissioner under this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Money collected.</span></h2>
          <p>Money collected by the commissioner as rents, charges, or fees in connection with and for the use of a parking lot or facility is appropriated to the commissioner for the purpose of operating, maintaining, improving, and replacing parking lots or facilities owned or operated by the state, including providing necessary and suitable uniforms for employees, and to carry out the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Legislative parking resolutions.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this section do not affect rules of parking adopted by resolution of the legislature during legislative sessions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/162/#laws.0.10.0">2001 c 162 s 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.64.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 64</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.58.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Electric vehicle charging.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may require that a user of a charging station located on the State Capitol complex used to charge an electric vehicle pay an electric service fee as determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.58.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.63.0">1984 c 544 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.30.0">1984 c 597 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.2.13.0">1984 c 655 art 2 s 13</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/572/#laws.0.9.0">1990 c 572 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/514/#laws.0.13.0">1992 c 514 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.6.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/359/#laws.0.10.0">1998 c 359 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.11.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.74.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 74</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.581">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.581 DISTINCTIVE TAX-EXEMPT LICENSE PLATES.</h1>
        <p>Vehicles owned or leased by the state of Minnesota must display distinctive tax-exempt license plates unless otherwise exempted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.012">168.012</a>. The commissioner shall design these distinctive plates subject to the approval of the registrar. An administrative fee of $20 and a license plate fee of $10 for two plates per vehicle or a license plate fee of $5 for one plate per trailer is paid at the time of registration. The license plate registration is valid for the life of the vehicle or until the vehicle is no longer owned or leased by the state of Minnesota.</p>
        <p>When the state of Minnesota applies for distinctive tax-exempt plates on vehicles previously owned by local units of government, it shall pay an administrative fee of $10 and a plate fee that covers the cost of replacement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.581.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.16.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
