      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.748"><b>16B.748</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.187">326B.187</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.749"><b>16B.749</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.191">326B.191</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.75"><b>16B.75</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.194">326B.194</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.76"><b>16B.76</b> MS 2006 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.13.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 13</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.85">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.85 RISK MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.85.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Alternatives to conventional insurance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may implement programs of insurance or alternatives to the purchase of conventional insurance. This authority does not extend to areas of risk subject to: (1) collective bargaining agreements, (2) plans established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.18">43A.18</a>, or (3) programs established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.541">176.541 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.611">176.611</a>, except for the Department of Administration. The mechanism for implementing possible alternatives to conventional insurance is the risk management fund created in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.85.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Risk management fund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All state agencies, political subdivisions, and the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, may, in cooperation with the commissioner, participate in insurance programs and other funding alternative programs provided by the risk management fund.</p>
          <p>(b) When an entity described in paragraph (a) enters into an insurance or self-insurance program, each entity shall contribute the appropriate share of its costs as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The money in the fund to pay claims arising from state activities and for administrative costs, including costs for the adjustment and defense of the claims, is appropriated to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) Interest earned from the investment of money in the fund shall be credited to the fund and be available to the commissioner for the expenditures authorized in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(e) The fund is exempt from the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152#stat.16A.152.4">16A.152, subdivision 4</a>. In the event that proceeds in the fund are insufficient to pay outstanding claims and associated administrative costs, the commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of management and budget, may assess entities participating in the fund amounts sufficient to pay the costs. The commissioner shall determine the proportionate share of the assessment of each entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.85.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) review the state's exposure to various types of potential risks in consultation with affected entities and advise them as to the reduction of risk and fiscal management of those losses;</p>
          <p>(2) be responsible for statewide risk management coordination, evaluation of funding and insuring alternatives, and the approval of all insurance purchases in consultation with affected entities;</p>
          <p>(3) identify ways to eliminate redundant efforts in the management of state risk management and insurance programs;</p>
          <p>(4) maintain the state risk management information system; and</p>
          <p>(5) administer and maintain the state risk management fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.85.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Competitive bidding.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may request bids from insurance carriers or negotiate with insurance carriers and may enter into contracts of insurance carriers that in the judgment of the division are best qualified to underwrite and service the insurance programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.85.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Risk management fund not considered insurance.</span></h2>
          <p>A state agency, including an entity defined as a part of the state in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.732#stat.3.732.1">3.732, subdivision 1</a>, clause (1), may procure insurance against liability of the agency and its employees for damages resulting from the torts of the agency and its employees. The procurement of this insurance constitutes a waiver of the limits of governmental liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736">3.736</a>, subdivisions 4 and 4a, only to the extent that valid and collectible insurance, including where applicable, proceeds from the Minnesota Insurance Guaranty Association, exceeds those limits and covers the claim. Purchase of insurance has no other effect on the liability of the agency and its employees. Procurement of commercial insurance, participation in the risk management fund under this section, or provisions of an individual self-insurance plan with or without a reserve fund or reinsurance does not constitute a waiver of any governmental immunities or exclusions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.85.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/455/#laws.0.3.0">1986 c 455 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/613/#laws.0.18.0">1988 c 613 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.4.32.0">1992 c 513 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.111.0">1993 c 192 s 111</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/634/#laws.1.17.0">1994 c 634 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/398/#laws.0.21.0">1996 c 398 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/162/#laws.0.7.0">2001 c 162 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.1.2.0">2006 c 212 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.7.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.86">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.86 STATE BUILDING ENERGY CONSERVATION IMPROVEMENT REVOLVING LOAN ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.86.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.87">16B.87</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Energy conservation" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2402#stat.216B.2402.5">216B.2402, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Energy conservation improvement" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2402#stat.216B.2402.6">216B.2402, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Energy efficiency" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2402#stat.216B.2402.7">216B.2402, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) "Project" means the energy conservation improvements financed by a loan made under this section.</p>
          <p>(f) "State building" means an existing building owned by the state of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.86.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The state building energy conservation improvement revolving loan account is established as a separate account in the state treasury. The commissioner shall manage the account and shall credit to the account investment income, repayments of principal and interest, and any other earnings arising from assets of the account. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of administration to make loans to state agencies to implement energy conservation and energy efficiency improvements in state buildings under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.87">16B.87</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.86.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.16.0">1987 c 365 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.11.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.87">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.87 AWARD AND REPAYMENT OF STATE BUILDING ENERGY IMPROVEMENT CONSERVATION LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.87.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>The State Building Energy Conservation Improvement Loan Committee consists of the commissioners of administration, management and budget, and commerce. The commissioner of administration serves as chair of the committee. The members serve without compensation or reimbursement for expenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.87.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Award and terms of loans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An agency shall apply for a loan on a form developed by the commissioner of administration that requires an applicant to submit the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the proposed project, including existing equipment, structural elements, operating characteristics, and other conditions affecting energy use that the energy conservation improvements financed by the loan modify or replace;</p>
          <p>(2) the total estimated project cost and the loan amount sought;</p>
          <p>(3) a detailed project budget;</p>
          <p>(4) projections of the proposed project's expected energy and monetary savings;</p>
          <p>(5) information demonstrating the agency's ability to repay the loan;</p>
          <p>(6) a description of the energy conservation programs offered by the utility providing service to the state building from which the applicant seeks additional funding for the project; and</p>
          <p>(7) any additional information requested by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The committee shall review applications for loans and shall award a loan based upon criteria adopted by the committee. A loan made under this section must:</p>
          <p>(1) be at or below the market rate of interest, including a zero interest loan; and</p>
          <p>(2) have a term no longer than ten years.</p>
          <p>(c) In making awards, the committee shall give preference to:</p>
          <p>(1) applicants that have sought funding for the project through energy conservation projects offered by the utility serving the state building that is the subject of the application; and</p>
          <p>(2) to the extent feasible, applications for state buildings located within the electric retail service area of the utility that is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116C.779">116C.779</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.87.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Repayment.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency receiving a loan under this section shall repay the loan according to the terms of the loan agreement. The principal and interest must be paid to the commissioner of administration, who shall deposit it in the state building energy conservation improvement revolving loan account. Payments of loan principal and interest must begin no later than one year after the project is completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.87.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.67.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 67</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/365/#laws.0.17.0">1987 c 365 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.6.0">2008 c 204 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.2.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.75.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 75</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.875">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.875 REVIEW BY COMMISSIONER OF ADMINISTRATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of administration shall review on a regular basis the duties and responsibilities of the various state departments, agencies and boards which have an operational effect upon the safety of the public, and recommend to the governor and the legislature such organizational and statutory policies as will best serve the purposes of Laws 1969, chapter 1129.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.875.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.1.1.0">1969 c 1129 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/5/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.35.0">1977 c 305 s 35</a>,36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.248.0">1981 c 356 s 248</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 289 s 115</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/10/#laws.0.87.0">1Sp1985 c 10 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.29.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.11.21.0">1995 c 248 art 11 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/366/#laws.0.68.0">1998 c 366 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/238/#laws.2.69.0">1999 c 238 art 2 s 69</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.89"><b>16B.89</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.1.35.0">1998 c 386 art 1 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.92"><b>16B.92</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/4A.05">4A.05</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.93"><b>16B.93</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.6.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.94"><b>16B.94</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.6.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 6</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.96"><b>16B.96</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.6.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 6</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GRANT PROCESS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.97">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.97 GRANTS MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant agreement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A grant agreement is a written instrument or electronic document defining a legal relationship between a granting agency and a grantee when the principal purpose of the relationship is to transfer cash or something of value to the recipient to support a public purpose authorized by law instead of acquiring by professional or technical contract, purchase, lease, or barter property or services for the direct benefit or use of the granting agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This section does not apply to general obligation grants as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a> and capital project grants to political subdivisions as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.86">16A.86</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants governance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall provide leadership and direction for policy related to grants management in Minnesota in order to foster more consistent, streamlined interaction between executive agencies, funders, and grantees that will enhance access to grant opportunities and information and lead to greater program accountability and transparency. The commissioner has the duties and powers stated in this section. Executive agencies shall fully cooperate with the commissioner in the creation, management, and oversight of state grants and must do what the commissioner requires under this section. The commissioner may adopt rules to carry out grants governance, oversight, and management.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.97.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Discretionary powers.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner has the authority to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) review grants management practices and establish and enforce policy and procedure improvements;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) sponsor, support, and facilitate innovative and collaborative grants management projects with public and private organizations;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) review, recommend, and implement alternative strategies for grants management;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) collect and disseminate information, issue reports relating to grants management, and sponsor and conduct conferences and studies;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) participate in conferences and other appropriate activities related to grants management issues;</p>
          <p>(6) suspend or debar grantees from eligibility to receive state-issued grants for up to three years for reasons specified in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1230.1150">1230.1150</a>, subpart 2. A grantee may obtain an administrative hearing pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a> before a suspension or debarment is effective by filing a written request for hearing within 20 days of notification of suspension or debarment;</p>
          <p>(7) establish offices for the purpose of carrying out grants governance, oversight, and management; and</p>
          <p>(8) require granting agencies to submit grant solicitation documents for review prior to issuance at dollar levels determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.97.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) create general grants management policies and procedures that are applicable to all executive agencies. The commissioner may approve exceptions to these policies and procedures for particular grant programs. Exceptions shall expire or be renewed after five years. Executive agencies shall retain management of individual grants programs;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) provide a central point of contact concerning statewide grants management policies and procedures;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) serve as a resource to executive agencies in such areas as training, evaluation, collaboration, and best practices in grants management;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) ensure grants management needs are considered in the development, upgrade, and use of statewide administrative systems and leverage existing technology wherever possible;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) oversee and approve future professional and technical service contracts and other information technology spending related to executive agency grants management systems and activities;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) provide a central point of contact for comments about executive agencies violating statewide grants governance policies and about fraud and waste in grants processes;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) forward received comments to the appropriate agency for further action, and may follow up as necessary;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) provide a single listing of all available executive agency competitive grant opportunities and resulting grant recipients;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) selectively review development and implementation of executive agency grants, policies, and practices; and</p>
          <p class="in">(10) selectively review executive agency compliance with best practices.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner may determine that it is cost-effective for agencies to develop and use shared grants management technology systems. This system would be governed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.01#stat.16E.01.3">16E.01, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.97.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Data classification.</span></h2>
          <p>Data maintained by the commissioner that identify a person providing comments to the commissioner under subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clauses (6) and (7), are private and nonpublic data but may be shared with the executive agency that is the subject of the comments.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.22.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/365/#laws.1.9.0">2010 c 365 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.9.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.7.2.0">2023 c 62 art 7 s 2</a>-4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.98">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.98 GRANTS MANAGEMENT PROCESS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) As a condition of receiving a grant from an appropriation of state funds, the recipient of the grant must agree to minimize administrative costs. The granting agency is responsible for negotiating appropriate limits to these costs so that the state derives the optimum benefit for grant funding.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to general obligation grants as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a> and also capital project grants to political subdivisions as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.86">16A.86</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Ethical practices and conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An employee of the executive branch involved directly or indirectly in grants processes, at any level, is subject to the code of ethics in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.38">43A.38</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner must develop policies regarding code of ethics and conflict of interest designed to prevent conflicts of interest for employees, committee members, or others involved in the recommendation, awarding, and administration of grants. The policies must apply to employees who are directly or indirectly in the grants process, which may include the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) developing request for proposals or evaluation criteria;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) drafting, recommending, awarding, amending, revising, or entering into grant agreements;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) evaluating or monitoring performance; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) authorizing payments.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The policies must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a process to make all parties to the grant aware of policies and laws relating to conflict of interest, and training on how to avoid and address potential conflicts; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a process under which those who have a conflict of interest or a potential conflict of interest must disclose the matter.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If the employee, appointing authority, or commissioner determines that a conflict of interest exists, the matter shall be assigned to another employee who does not have a conflict of interest. If it is not possible to assign the matter to an employee who does not have a conflict of interest, interested personnel shall be notified of the conflict and the employee may proceed with the assignment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reporting of violations.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A state employee who discovers evidence of violation of laws or rules governing grants is encouraged to report the violation or suspected violation to the employee's supervisor, the commissioner or the commissioner's designee, or the legislative auditor. The legislative auditor shall report to the Legislative Audit Commission if there are multiple complaints about the same agency. The auditor's report to the Legislative Audit Commission under this section must disclose only the number and type of violations alleged. An employee making a good faith report under this section has the protections provided for under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/181.932">181.932</a>, prohibiting the employer from discriminating against the employee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Creation and validity of grant agreements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A grant agreement and amendments are not valid and do not bind unless:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the grant agreement and amendments have been executed by the head of the agency or a delegate who is party to the grant;</p>
          <p>(2) the grant agreement and amendments have been approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the accounting system shows an encumbrance for the amount of the grant in accordance with policy approved by the commissioner except as provided in subdivision 11; and</p>
          <p>(4) the grant agreement and amendments include an effective date that references either section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05#stat.16C.05.2">16C.05, subdivision 2</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98, subdivisions 5</a> and 7, as determined by the granting agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The combined grant agreement and amendments must not exceed five years without specific, written approval by the commissioner according to established policy, procedures, and standards, or unless the commissioner determines that a longer duration is in the best interest of the state.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A fully executed copy of the grant agreement with all amendments and other required records relating to the grant must be kept on file at the granting agency for a time equal to that required of grantees in subdivision 8.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Grant agreements must comply with policies established by the commissioner for minimum grant agreement standards and practices.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The attorney general may periodically review and evaluate a sample of state agency grants to ensure compliance with applicable laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grant administration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A granting agency shall diligently administer and monitor any grant it has entered into. A granting agency must report to the commissioner at any time at the commissioner's request on the status of any grant to which the agency is a party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Grant payments.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Payments to the grantee may not be issued until the grant agreement is fully executed. Encumbrances for grants issued by June 30 may be certified for a period of one year beyond the year in which the funds were originally appropriated as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.28#stat.16A.28.6">16A.28, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Audit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A grant agreement made by an executive agency must include an audit clause that provides that the books, records, documents, and accounting procedures and practices of the grantee or other party that are relevant to the grant or transaction are subject to examination by the commissioner, the granting agency and either the legislative auditor or the state auditor, as appropriate, for a minimum of six years from the grant agreement end date, receipt and approval of all final reports, or the required period of time to satisfy all state and program retention requirements, whichever is later. If a grant agreement does not include an express audit clause, the audit authority under this subdivision is implied.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the granting agency is a local unit of government, and the governing body of the local unit of government requests that the state auditor examine the books, records, documents, and accounting procedures and practices of the grantee or other party according to this subdivision, the granting agency shall be liable for the cost of the examination. If the granting agency is a local unit of government, and the grantee or other party requests that the state auditor examine all books, records, documents, and accounting procedures and practices related to the grant, the grantee or other party that requested the examination shall be liable for the cost of the examination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Authority of attorney general.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The attorney general may pursue remedies available by law to avoid the obligation of an agency to pay under a grant or to recover payments made if activities under the grant are so unsatisfactory, incomplete, or inconsistent that payment would involve unjust enrichment. The contrary opinion of the granting agency does not affect the power of the attorney general under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Grants with Indian tribes and bands.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding any other law, an agency may not require an Indian tribe or band to deny its sovereignty as a requirement or condition of a grant with an agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Encumbrance exception.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 5, paragraph (a), clause (2), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05#stat.16C.05.2">16C.05, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), clause (3), agencies may permit a specifically named, legislatively appropriated, noncompetitive grant recipient to incur eligible expenses based on an agreed upon work plan and budget for up to 60 days prior to an encumbrance being established in the accounting system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Grantee evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The head of the agency or delegate entering into a grant agreement in excess of $25,000 must submit a report to the commissioner who must make the report publicly available online.</p>
          <p>(b) The report must:</p>
          <p>(1) summarize the purpose of the grant;</p>
          <p>(2) state the amount provided to the grantee; and</p>
          <p>(3) include a written performance evaluation of the work done under the grant. The evaluation must include an appraisal of the grantee's timeliness, quality, and overall performance in meeting the terms and objectives of the grant. Grantees may request copies of evaluations prepared under this subdivision and may respond in writing. Grantee responses must be maintained with the grant file.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Limitations on actions.</span></h2>
          <p>No action may be maintained by a grantee against an employee or agency who discloses information about a current or former grantee under subdivision 12, unless the grantee demonstrates by clear and convincing evidence that:</p>
          <p>(1) the information was false and defamatory;</p>
          <p>(2) the employee or agency knew or should have known the information was false and acted with malicious intent to injure the current or former grantee; and</p>
          <p>(3) the information was acted upon in a manner that caused harm to the current or former grantee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.98.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Administrative costs.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless amounts are otherwise appropriated for administrative costs, a state agency may retain up to five percent of the amount appropriated to the agency for grants enacted by the legislature and formula grants and up to ten percent for competitively awarded grants. This subdivision applies to appropriations made for new grant programs enacted on or after July 1, 2023. This subdivision does not apply to grants funded with an appropriation of proceeds from the sale of state general obligation bonds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.23.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/264/#laws.5.1.0">2012 c 264 art 5 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/187/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 187 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.10.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.7.5.0">2023 c 62 art 7 s 5</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/69/#laws.0.9.0">2023 c 69 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.981">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.981 FINANCIAL REVIEW OF GRANT AND BUSINESS SUBSIDY RECIPIENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Grant" means a grant of $50,000 or more as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97, subdivision </a>1, paragraph (a); or business subsidy of $50,000 or more as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.994">116J.994</a>, subdivision 3, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) "Grantee" means a political subdivision, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345, subdivision </a>1; a nonprofit, as defined in chapter 317A; or a business entity, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.001">5.001</a>, subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Financial information required; determination of ability to perform.</span></h2>
          <p>For grants of $50,000 or more and subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a>, before an agency awards a competitive, legislatively named, single-source, or sole-source grant, the agency must complete a preaward risk assessment to assess the risk that a potential grantee cannot or would not perform the required duties. In making this assessment, the agency must review the following information as applicable:</p>
          <p>(1) the potential grantee's history of performing duties similar to those required by the grant, whether the grant requires the potential grantee to perform services at a significantly increased scale, and whether the grant will require significant changes to the operation of the potential grantee's organization;</p>
          <p>(2) for a potential grantee that is a nonprofit organization, the potential grantee's most recent Form 990 or Form 990-EZ filed with the Internal Revenue Service. If the potential grantee has not been in existence long enough or is not required to file Form 990 or Form 990-EZ, the potential grantee must demonstrate to the agency's satisfaction that the potential grantee is exempt and must instead submit the potential grantee's most recent board-reviewed financial statements and documentation of internal controls or, if there is no such board, by the applicant's managing group;</p>
          <p>(3) for a potential grantee that is a for-profit business, the potential grantee's most recent federal and state tax returns, current financial statements, certification that the business is not under bankruptcy proceedings, and disclosure of any liens on its assets. If a business has not been in business long enough to have a tax return, the grantee must demonstrate to the agency's satisfaction that the grantee has appropriate internal financial controls;</p>
          <p>(4) evidence of good standing with the secretary of state under chapter 317A, or other applicable law;</p>
          <p>(5) if the potential grantee is required to complete an audit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/309.53">309.53</a>, subdivision 3, the potential grantee's most recent audit report performed by an independent third party in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles; and</p>
          <p>(6) certification, provided by the potential grantee, that none of its current principals have been convicted of a felony financial crime in the last ten years. For this section, a principal is defined as a public official, a board member, or staff with the authority to access funds provided by this agency or determine how those funds are used.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Additional measures for some grantees.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency may require additional information and may provide enhanced oversight for grantees that have not previously received state or federal grants for similar amounts or similar duties and have not yet demonstrated the ability to perform the duties required under the grant on the scale required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agency authority to not award grant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, while performing the required steps in subdivision 2 and pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.991">16B.991</a>, the agency requires additional information to determine whether there is a substantial risk that the potential grantee cannot or would not perform the required duties of the grant agreement, the agency must give the grantee 30 business days within which the grantee can respond to the agency for the purpose of satisfying the agency's concerns or work with the agency to develop a plan to satisfy the concerns.</p>
          <p>(b) If, after performing the required steps in subdivision 2 and pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.991">16B.991</a>, and after reviewing any additional requested information from the grantee, the agency still has concerns that there is a substantial risk that a potential grantee cannot or would not perform the required duties under the grant agreement, the agency must either create a plan to satisfy remaining concerns with the grantee or must not award the grant.</p>
          <p>(c) If, pursuant to paragraphs (a) and (b), the agency does not award a competitive, single-source, or sole-source grant, the agency must provide notification to the grantee and the commissioner of administration of the determination. The notification to the grantee must include the agency's reason for postponing or forgoing the grant, including information sufficient to explain and support the agency's decision, and notify the applicant of the process for contesting the agency's decision with the agency and the applicant's options under paragraph (d). If the applicant contests the agency's decision no later than 15 business days after receiving the notice, the agency must consider any additional written information submitted by the grantee. The agency has 15 business days to consider this information, during which the agency may reverse or modify the agency's initial decision to postpone or forgo the grant.</p>
          <p>(d) The final decision by an agency under paragraph (c) may be challenged as a contested case under chapter 14. The contested case proceeding must be initiated within 30 business days of the date of written notification of a final decision by the agency.</p>
          <p>(e) If, pursuant to paragraphs (a) and (b), the agency does not award a legislatively named grant, the agency must delay award of the grant until adjournment of the next regular or special legislative session for action from the legislature. The agency must provide notification to the potential grantee, the commissioner of administration, and the chairs and ranking minority members of the Ways and Means Committee in the house of representatives and the chairs and ranking minority members of the Finance Committee in the senate. The notification to the grantee must include the agency's reason for postponing or forgoing the grant, including information sufficient to explain and support the agency's decision and notify the applicant of the process for contesting the agency's decision. If the applicant contests the agency's decision no later than 15 business days after receiving the notice, the agency must consider any additional written information submitted by the grantee. The agency has 15 business days to consider this information, during which the agency may reverse or modify the agency's initial decision to postpone or forgo the grant. The notification to the commissioner of administration and legislators must identify the legislatively named potential grantee and the agency's reason for postponing or forgoing the grant. After hearing the concerns of the agency, the legislature may reaffirm the award of the grant or reappropriate the funds to a different legislatively named grantee. Based on the action of the legislature, the agency must award the grant to the legislatively named grantee. If the legislature does not provide direction to the agency on the disposition of the grant, the funds revert to the original appropriation source.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Authority to award subject to additional assistance and oversight.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency that identifies an area of significant concern regarding an applicant's financial standing or management may award a grant to the applicant if the agency provides or the potential grantee otherwise obtains necessary technical assistance. If the agency cannot provide and the grantee cannot otherwise reasonably obtain necessary technical assistance, the agency may award the grant if the agency establishes additional requirements in the grant agreement. Additional requirements may include but are not limited to enhanced monitoring, additional reporting, or other reasonable requirements imposed by the agency to protect the interests of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.981.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grants with Indian Tribes and bands.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law, an agency may not require an Indian Tribe or band to deny its sovereignty as a requirement or condition of a grant with an agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.981.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.7.11.0">2023 c 62 art 7 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16B.99"><b>16B.99</b> MS 2010 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.30">16E.30</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16B.991">
        <h1 class="shn">16B.991 TERMINATION OF GRANT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.991.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Criminal conviction.</span></h2>
          <p>Each grant agreement subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a> must provide that the agreement will immediately be terminated if the recipient is convicted of a criminal offense relating to a state grant agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16B.991.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>A grant agreement must by its terms permit the commissioner to unilaterally terminate the grant agreement prior to completion if the commissioner determines that further performance under the grant agreement would not serve agency purposes or is not in the best interests of the state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16B.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.2.23.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.7.12.0">2023 c 62 art 7 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
